解读肛管鳞状细胞癌的分子景观:从生物学到精准肿瘤学

Deciphering the Molecular Landscape of Squamous Cell Carcinoma of the Anal Canal: From Biology to Precision Oncology
作者： Matilde Callegarin, Valentina Angerilli, Jessica Gasparello, Francesca Bergamo, Rodrigo Humberto Girón Cuestas, Paola Parente, Sara Lonardi, Matteo Fassan
摘要中文翻译肛管鳞状细胞癌(SCAC)是一种罕见的恶性肿瘤,其发病率在全球范围内一直在稳步增长。高危型人乳头瘤病毒(HPV),特别是HPV16和HPV18基因型的持续感染,是主要的病因,在肿瘤的发生发展中发挥着核心作用。虽然联合放化疗仍然是局部疾病的标准 治疗 并实现了较高的肿瘤控制率,但相当一部分患者出现复发或出现晚期疾病。对于后者,治疗选择仍然有限。在过去的十年中,基因组分析的进步极大地扩展了我们对 SCAC 生物学的理解。影响 PI3K/AKT/mTOR 通路的反复改变,尤其是 PIK3CA 突变,已成为最常见的分子事件,特别是在 HPV 阳性肿瘤中。其他改变涉及受体酪氨酸激酶信号传导、染色质重塑基因、DNA 损伤反应途径和 MAPK 级联的组成部分。此外,HPV 阳性和 HPV 阴性肿瘤表现出不同的分子特征,具有重要的预后意义。尽管仍然缺乏可靠的预测生物标志物,但免疫疗法最近已成为 治疗 治疗晚期 SCAC 的重要组成部分。这篇综述总结了 SCAC 分子格局的当前证据,讨论了新兴的预后和预测生物标志物,并强调了开发更个性化治疗策略的潜在机会。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述重点关注肛管鳞状细胞癌 (SCAC) 的分子概况,重点关注 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 的作用,特别是 高危型HPV 类型,例如 HPV16 和 HPV18。核心问题是了解 SCAC 发生和进展的分子机制,并探索这些发现对精准肿瘤学的影响。
证据基础与方法
该综述是对 SCAC 分子特征现有文献的叙述性综合,重点关注 HPV 相关机制。它不报告特定的数据库、搜索日期或荟萃分析方法。证据来自该领域的一般观察和发现,没有明确提及研究选择标准或定量分析。
共识与结论
综述认为,高危型HPV,尤其是HPV16和HPV18的持续感染是SCAC的主要病因。它强调,PI3K/AKT/mTOR 通路的反复改变,特别是 PIK3CA 突变,是 HPV 阳性肿瘤中最常见的分子事件。此外,HPV 阳性和 HPV 阴性肿瘤表现出不同的分子特征,具有重要的预后意义。
争议与缺口
该综述指出,虽然联合放化疗是局部 SCAC 的标准 治疗,但相当一部分患者会出现复发或出现晚期疾病。它还提到晚期疾病的治疗选择仍然有限。此外,该综述还发现,晚期 SCAC 免疫治疗缺乏可靠的预测生物标志物。
科研与实践启示
该综述强调需要进一步研究 SCAC 的分子机制,特别是在 HPV 阳性肿瘤中,以开发更个性化的治疗策略。它还强调了识别免疫治疗的可靠预测生物标志物以及改进 监测 和 HPV 相关癌症的诊断方法的重要性。

Deciphering the Molecular Landscape of Squamous Cell Carcinoma of the Anal Canal: From Biology to Precision Oncology

Authors: Matilde Callegarin, Valentina Angerilli, Jessica Gasparello, Francesca Bergamo, Rodrigo Humberto Girón Cuestas, Paola Parente, Sara Lonardi, Matteo Fassan
Original abstract (English)
Squamous cell carcinoma of the anal canal (SCAC) is a rare malignancy whose incidence has been steadily increasing worldwide. Persistent infection with high-risk human papillomavirus (HPV), particularly HPV16 and HPV18 genotypes, is the main etiological factor and plays a central role in tumor development. While combined chemoradiotherapy remains the standard treatment for localized disease and achieves high rates of tumor control, a considerable proportion of patients experience recurrence or present with advanced disease. For the latter, therapeutic options remain limited. Over the last decade, advances in genomic profiling have significantly expanded our understanding of SCAC biology. Recurrent alterations affecting the PI3K/AKT/mTOR pathway, especially PIK3CA mutations, have emerged as the most common molecular events, particularly in HPV-positive tumors. Additional alterations involve receptor tyrosine kinase signaling, chromatin remodeling genes, DNA damage response pathways, and components of the MAPK cascade. Moreover, HPV-positive and HPV-negative tumors display distinct molecular features with important prognostic implications. Immunotherapy has recently become an important component of treatment for advanced SCAC, although reliable predictive biomarkers are still lacking. This review summarizes the current evidence on the molecular landscape of SCAC, discusses emerging prognostic and predictive biomarkers, and highlights potential opportunities for the development of more personalized therapeutic strategies.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review focuses on the molecular landscape of squamous cell carcinoma of the anal canal (SCAC), with an emphasis on the role of human papillomaviruses (HPV), particularly high-risk HPV types such as HPV16 and HPV18. The central question is to understand the molecular mechanisms underlying SCAC development and progression, and to explore the implications of these findings for precision oncology.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative synthesis of existing literature on the molecular features of SCAC, with a focus on HPV-related mechanisms. It does not report specific databases, search dates, or meta-analysis methods. The evidence is derived from general observations and findings in the field, with no explicit mention of study selection criteria or quantitative analysis.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that persistent infection with high-risk HPV, especially HPV16 and HPV18, is the main etiological factor in SCAC. It highlights that recurrent alterations in the PI3K/AKT/mTOR pathway, particularly PIK3CA mutations, are the most common molecular events in HPV-positive tumors. Additionally, HPV-positive and HPV-negative tumors exhibit distinct molecular features with important prognostic implications.
Controversies and gaps
The review notes that while combined chemoradiotherapy is the standard treatment for localized SCAC, a considerable proportion of patients experience recurrence or present with advanced disease. It also mentions that therapeutic options for advanced disease remain limited. Furthermore, the review identifies a lack of reliable predictive biomarkers for immunotherapy in advanced SCAC.
Research and practice implications
The review emphasizes the need for further research into the molecular mechanisms of SCAC, particularly in HPV-positive tumors, to develop more personalized therapeutic strategies. It also highlights the importance of identifying reliable predictive biomarkers for immunotherapy and improving surveillance and diagnostic methods for HPV-associated cancers.

阴道惰性乳杆菌与宫颈癌关系的研究进展

Research progress on the association between vaginal Lactobacillus iners and cervical cancer
作者： Xiaodan Zhang
摘要中文翻译高危人乳头瘤病毒(hrHPV)持续感染是宫颈癌的核心病因,而宿主阴道微环境在疾病进展中起着关键作用。惰性乳杆菌是阴道内分布最广泛的乳杆菌之一。由于其独特的生物学特性,包括独家生产L-乳酸和小基因组,它在阴道生态系统中占据过渡状态。最近的研究表明,惰性乳杆菌(L. iners)在宫颈癌的发生和进展中发挥着双重且矛盾的作用。在阴道微环境中,L. iners 与持续的 hrHPV 感染和宫颈上皮内瘤变 (CIN) 进展的风险增加有关;其保护作用有限,并且常常与不利的炎症状态共存。相比之下,在肿瘤微环境中,L. iners在瘤内定植可显着增加L-乳酸的产生,诱导肿瘤细胞对放化疗产生耐药性,并成为不良预后的独立危险因素。本综述系统总结了L. iners的生物学特性、其在宫颈癌起始、进展和治疗耐药中的作用和机制,及其作为新型生物标志物和潜在治疗靶点的转化价值。还讨论了当前的争议、局限性和未来的方向。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述在持续高危 人乳头瘤病毒 (hrHPV) 感染的背景下检查了惰性乳杆菌和 宫颈癌 之间的关联,重点关注阴道和肿瘤微环境作为疾病进展和 治疗 反应的关键决定因素。
证据基础与方法
该综述综合了惰性乳杆菌在 宫颈癌 起始、进展和 治疗 耐药性中的生物学特征、作用和机制,明确讨论了当前的争议、局限性和未来方向,但没有报告数据库、检索日期、资格标准或研究计数。
共识与结论
持续性hrHPV感染是宫颈癌的核心病因,而包括惰性乳杆菌在内的阴道微环境在疾病进展中发挥着关键作用。惰性乳杆菌由于其独特的生物学特性,例如独家生产L-乳酸和小型基因组,在阴道生态系统中占据过渡状态。
争议与缺口
惰性乳杆菌在宫颈癌发生中表现出矛盾的作用,与持续的hrHPV感染和阴道微环境中宫颈上皮内瘤变(CIN)进展的风险增加有关,而其瘤内定植可增加L-乳酸的产生,诱导放化疗耐药,并作为肿瘤微环境中预后不良的独立危险因素。
科研与实践启示
该综述强调了惰性乳杆菌作为 宫颈癌 的新型生物标志物和潜在治疗靶点的转化价值,强调需要澄清其在不同微环境中的双重作用,为诊断和治疗策略提供信息。

Research progress on the association between vaginal Lactobacillus iners and cervical cancer

Authors: Xiaodan Zhang
Original abstract (English)
Persistent infection with high-risk Human Papillomavirus (hrHPV) is the central etiological factor in cervical cancer, while the host vaginal microenvironment plays a critical role in disease progression. Lactobacillus iners is one of the most widely distributed lactobacilli in the vagina. Because of its unique biological features, including exclusive production of L-lactic acid and a small genome, it occupies a transitional state within the vaginal ecosystem. Recent studies have shown that Lactobacillus iners (L. iners) plays dual and paradoxical roles in cervical carcinogenesis and progression. In the vaginal microenvironment, L. iners is associated with persistent hrHPV infection and an increased risk of Cervical Intraepithelial Neoplasia (CIN) progression; its protective effects are limited and often coexist with unfavorable inflammatory states. In contrast, within the tumor microenvironment, intratumoral colonization by L. iners can markedly increase L-lactate production, induce chemoradiotherapy resistance in tumor cells, and serve as an independent risk factor for poor prognosis. This review systematically summarizes the biological characteristics of L. iners, its roles and mechanisms in cervical cancer initiation, progression, and treatment resistance, and its translational value as a novel biomarker and potential therapeutic target. Current controversies, limitations, and future directions are also discussed.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines the association between Lactobacillus iners and cervical cancer within the context of persistent high-risk human papillomavirus (hrHPV) infection, focusing on the vaginal and tumor microenvironments as critical determinants of disease progression and treatment response.
Evidence base and method
The review synthesizes biological characteristics, roles, and mechanisms of Lactobacillus iners in cervical cancer initiation, progression, and treatment resistance, explicitly discussing current controversies, limitations, and future directions without reporting databases, search dates, eligibility criteria, or study counts.
Consensus and conclusions
Persistent hrHPV infection is the central etiological factor in cervical cancer, while the vaginal microenvironment, including Lactobacillus iners, plays a critical role in disease progression. Lactobacillus iners occupies a transitional state in the vaginal ecosystem due to its unique biological features, such as exclusive production of L-lactic acid and a small genome.
Controversies and gaps
Lactobacillus iners exhibits paradoxical roles in cervical carcinogenesis, being associated with persistent hrHPV infection and increased risk of Cervical Intraepithelial Neoplasia (CIN) progression in the vaginal microenvironment, while its intratumoral colonization can increase L-lactate production, induce chemoradiotherapy resistance, and serve as an independent risk factor for poor prognosis in the tumor microenvironment.
Research and practice implications
The review highlights the translational value of Lactobacillus iners as a novel biomarker and potential therapeutic target for cervical cancer, emphasizing the need to clarify its dual roles in different microenvironments to inform diagnostic and therapeutic strategies.

德克萨斯州南部社区确定的改善 HPV 疫苗接种 的障碍和策略

Community-Identified Barriers and Strategies for Improving HPV Vaccination in South Texas
作者： Erika L. Thompson, Miranda Cano, Corin Reyes, Tharani Ravi, Jasmine Rodriguez, Allison Grimes
摘要中文翻译贝克萨尔县是德克萨斯州南部的一个县,人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 疫苗接种 率较低,这阻碍了预防 HPV 相关癌症的努力。我们从贝克萨尔县管理社会/卫生服务和/或联盟的社区成员的角度进行了社区评估。我们的目的是评估贝克萨尔县针对 HPV 疫苗接种 的已知障碍和可能的社区解决方案。使用在线定量调查收集数据,测量基于增加的 疫苗接种 模型对 HPV 疫苗接种 的知识和态度,以及对 HPV 疫苗接种 的感知障碍和改善 疫苗接种 的潜在解决方案。参与者包括 2025 年 1 月至 3 月贝克萨尔县社会/卫生服务/部分联盟的管理人员 (N = 45)。估计了描述性统计数据。大多数参与者强烈同意 HPV 疫苗接种 率可以提高,并且对于 9 至 26 岁的人来说,接种 HPV 疫苗非常重要。组织层面的主要障碍是相互竞争的健康优先事项、缺乏提供者知识/培训、语言障碍和患者缺乏意识。参与者被要求考虑可以采取哪些社区层面的策略来增加 HPV 疫苗接种;评分最高的是移动 疫苗接种、公众意识活动以及 疫苗 融资。社区对改善 Bexar 县的 HPV 疫苗接种 表现出浓厚的兴趣。制定解决直接获取问题的多成分干预措施,再加上提供者的建议,可以克服该社区中报告的 HPV 疫苗接种 障碍。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV),旨在找出改善南德克萨斯州贝克萨尔县 HPV 疫苗接种 率的障碍和策略,特别是在 9 至 26 岁的人群中。核心问题是了解社区层面的挑战和增强 HPV 疫苗接种 的潜在解决方案。
证据基础与方法
该审查使用在线定量调查对贝克萨尔县管理社会/卫生服务或联盟成员的个人进行了社区评估。数据收集时间为 2025 年 1 月至 3 月,涉及 45 名参与者。估计描述性统计数据以分析结果。
共识与结论
审查发现,大多数参与者强烈同意 HPV 疫苗接种 率可以提高,并且对于 9 至 26 岁的人来说接种疫苗非常重要。主要结论是,解决直接获取问题和提供者建议的多成分干预措施可以克服​​社区中 HPV 疫苗接种 所报告的障碍。
争议与缺口
该审查确定了组织层面的主要障碍,包括相互竞争的健康优先事项、缺乏提供者知识/培训、语言障碍和患者缺乏意识。然而,证据并未报告与 HPV 相关的具体争议或未解决的机制。
科研与实践启示
该审查强调了社区层面策略的必要性,例如移动 疫苗接种、公众意识活动和 疫苗 融资,以提高 HPV 疫苗接种 率。它还表明解决准入问题和提供者培训以加强 疫苗接种 工作的重要性。

Community-Identified Barriers and Strategies for Improving HPV Vaccination in South Texas

Authors: Erika L. Thompson, Miranda Cano, Corin Reyes, Tharani Ravi, Jasmine Rodriguez, Allison Grimes
Original abstract (English)
Bexar County is a South Texas county with low human papillomavirus (HPV) vaccination rates, which hinders efforts to prevent HPV-related cancers. We conducted a community assessment from the perspective of community members who administer social/health services and/or coalitions in Bexar County. We aimed to assess perceived barriers and possible community solutions to HPV vaccination in Bexar County. Data were collected using an online quantitative survey measuring knowledge and attitudes toward HPV vaccination based on the Increasing Vaccination Model, as well as perceived barriers to HPV vaccination and potential solutions to improve vaccination. Participants included persons administering social/health services/part of coalitions in Bexar County from January to March 2025 ( N = 45). Descriptive statistics were estimated. Most participants strongly agreed that HPV vaccination rates could be improved and that it was very important for 9- to 26-year-olds to get vaccinated for HPV. Primary barriers at the organizational level were competing health priorities, lack of provider knowledge/training, language barriers, and patient lack of awareness. Participants were asked to consider what community-level strategies could be deployed to increase HPV vaccination; the highest rated were mobile vaccination, public awareness campaigns, and financing for the vaccine. There is a strong community interest in improving HPV vaccination in Bexar County. Developing a multi-component intervention that addresses direct access issues, coupled with provider recommendations, could surmount the reported barriers to HPV vaccination in this community.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on human papillomaviruses (HPV) and aims to identify barriers and strategies for improving HPV vaccination rates in Bexar County, South Texas, particularly among 9- to 26-year-olds. The central question is about understanding community-level challenges and potential solutions to enhance HPV vaccination.
Evidence base and method
The review conducted a community assessment using an online quantitative survey targeting individuals who administer social/health services or are part of coalitions in Bexar County. Data were collected from January to March 2025, involving 45 participants. Descriptive statistics were estimated to analyze the findings.
Consensus and conclusions
The review found that most participants strongly agreed that HPV vaccination rates could be improved and that it was very important for 9- to 26-year-olds to get vaccinated. The main conclusion is that a multi-component intervention addressing direct access issues and provider recommendations could overcome the reported barriers to HPV vaccination in the community.
Controversies and gaps
The review identified primary barriers at the organizational level, including competing health priorities, lack of provider knowledge/training, language barriers, and patient lack of awareness. However, the evidence does not report on specific controversies or unresolved mechanisms related to HPV.
Research and practice implications
The review highlights the need for community-level strategies such as mobile vaccination, public awareness campaigns, and financing for the vaccine to improve HPV vaccination rates. It also suggests the importance of addressing access issues and provider training to enhance vaccination efforts.

用于子宫内膜和 宫颈癌 微小残留疾病评估的液体活检:分子原理、临床证据和转化障碍

Liquid Biopsy for Minimal Residual Disease Assessment in Endometrial and Cervical Cancers: Molecular Rationale, Clinical Evidence, and Translational Barriers
作者： Ludovica Pepe, Valeria Zuccalà, Walter Giuseppe Giordano, Giordana Di Mauro, Vincenzo Cianci, Cristina Mondello, Massimiliano Berretta, Vincenzo Fiorentino, Antonio Ieni
摘要中文翻译子宫内膜和 宫颈癌 可能会因常规 监测 上不明显的亚临床疾病而复发。这篇叙述性综述批判性地评价了液体活检用于微小残留病(MRD)评估和复发监测的作用。治疗 人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)后的宫颈癌中的循环肿瘤DNA(ctDNA)具有最强的疾病特异性临床有效性前瞻性证据;持续检测与复发密切相关,但中等敏感性和假阴性结果并不支持 治疗 仅根据阴性情况进行降级。对于子宫内膜癌,围手术期 ctDNA 得到了 11 项研究、1298 名患者的荟萃分析和额外的 队列研究 的预后支持,尽管检测异质性和有限的独立复制限制了临床准备。术后阳性通常表现出比术前检测更强的关联性。宫颈阴道和尿液 DNA 甲基化研究为检测已确定的复发(尤其是局部复发)提供了初步证据,但没有提供预期的分子前置时间或临床效用。数字 PCR、疾病特异性固定试剂盒、肿瘤信息检测和纠错测序服务于不同的环境;广泛的全癌血浆分析仍然主要是一种晚期疾病工具。循环肿瘤细胞、细胞外囊泡、microRNA、肿瘤培养的血小板和片段组学仍处于探索阶段。液体活检应继续作为研究的辅助手段,直到前瞻性试验表明根据分子发现采取行动可以改善结果。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述在液体活检的背景下检查了 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV),特别是高危类型(例如 HPV16、HPV18、HPV31、HPV33、HPV45、HPV52、HPV58)及其相关疾病(例如宫颈上皮内瘤变、HPV 相关 宫颈癌、尖锐湿疣),以进行微小残留病 (MRD) 评估和复发监控。核心问题集中在 HPV 特异性 ctDNA 和其他液体活检生物标志物在 治疗 监测 后和预后中的临床有效性和实用性。
证据基础与方法
这是一篇叙述性综述,评估用于 HPV 相关癌症 MRD 评估的液体活检方法。它综合了 ctDNA、循环肿瘤细胞、细胞外囊泡、microRNA、肿瘤教育血小板和片段组学的证据,强调了它们在宫颈癌和子宫内膜癌中的作用。该综述没有明确报告系统检索日期、数据库或资格标准。
共识与结论
治疗 后的 宫颈癌 中的 HPV 循环肿瘤 DNA (ctDNA) 显示出最强的疾病特异性临床有效性前瞻性证据;持续检测与复发密切相关。然而,中等敏感性和假阴性结果排除了仅基于阴性结果的 治疗 降级。在子宫内膜癌中,围手术期 ctDNA 得到了 11 项研究、1298 名患者的荟萃分析和额外的 队列研究 的预后支持,尽管检测异质性和有限的独立复制限制了临床准备。术后 ctDNA 阳性通常表现出比术前检测更强的与结果的关联。
争议与缺口
子宫内膜癌中 ctDNA 的检测异质性和有限的独立复制限制了临床准备。对于 宫颈癌,ctDNA 检测的中等灵敏度和假阴性结果限制了其在 治疗 降级中的效用。宫颈阴道和尿液 DNA 甲基化研究为检测复发提供了初步证据,但缺乏前瞻性的分子前置时间或临床实用性。循环肿瘤细胞、细胞外囊泡、microRNA、肿瘤培养血小板和片段组学仍处于探索阶段,尚未确定临床有效性。
科研与实践启示
液体活检应继续作为研究的辅助手段,直到前瞻性试验证明根据分子发现采取行动可以改善患者的预后。未来的研究应优先考虑标准化、疾病特异性检测,并具有经过验证的临床决策阈值,特别是针对 HPV 相关的宫颈癌和子宫内膜癌。需要进行前瞻性研究来确定 ctDNA 和其他生物标志物在指导 治疗 调整和 监测 策略方面的临床效用。

Liquid Biopsy for Minimal Residual Disease Assessment in Endometrial and Cervical Cancers: Molecular Rationale, Clinical Evidence, and Translational Barriers

Authors: Ludovica Pepe, Valeria Zuccalà, Walter Giuseppe Giordano, Giordana Di Mauro, Vincenzo Cianci, Cristina Mondello, Massimiliano Berretta, Vincenzo Fiorentino, Antonio Ieni
Original abstract (English)
Endometrial and cervical cancers can recur from subclinical disease not evident on routine surveillance. This narrative review critically evaluates liquid biopsy for minimal residual disease (MRD) assessment and recurrence monitoring. Post-treatment human papillomavirus (HPV) circulating tumor DNA (ctDNA) in cervical cancer has the strongest disease-specific prospective evidence of clinical validity; persistent detection is strongly associated with recurrence, but moderate sensitivity and false-negative results do not support treatment de-escalation on negativity alone. With regard to endometrial cancer, perioperative ctDNA has prognostic support from an 11-study, 1298-patient meta-analysis and additional cohorts, although assay heterogeneity and limited independent replication constrain clinical readiness. Postoperative positivity generally shows stronger associations than preoperative detection. Cervicovaginal and urine DNA-methylation studies provide preliminary evidence for detecting established recurrence, especially local recurrence, but not prospective molecular lead time or clinical utility. Digital PCR, disease-specific fixed panels, tumor-informed assays, and error-corrected sequencing serve distinct settings; broad pan-cancer plasma profiling remains mainly an advanced-disease tool. Circulating tumor cells, extracellular vesicles, microRNAs, tumor-educated platelets, and fragmentomics remain exploratory. Liquid biopsy should remain an investigational adjunct until prospective trials show that acting on molecular findings improves outcomes.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines human papillomaviruses (HPV), specifically high-risk types (e.g., HPV16, HPV18, HPV31, HPV33, HPV45, HPV52, HPV58) and their associated diseases (e.g., cervical intraepithelial neoplasia, HPV-associated cervical cancer, genital warts) in the context of liquid biopsy for minimal residual disease (MRD) assessment and recurrence monitoring. The central question focuses on the clinical validity and utility of HPV-specific ctDNA and other liquid biopsy biomarkers in post-treatment surveillance and prognosis.
Evidence base and method
This is a narrative review that evaluates liquid biopsy methods for MRD assessment in HPV-associated cancers. It synthesizes evidence on ctDNA, circulating tumor cells, extracellular vesicles, microRNAs, tumor-educated platelets, and fragmentomics, highlighting their roles in cervical and endometrial cancers. The review does not report systematic search dates, databases, or eligibility criteria explicitly.
Consensus and conclusions
Post-treatment HPV circulating tumor DNA (ctDNA) in cervical cancer shows the strongest disease-specific prospective evidence of clinical validity; persistent detection is strongly associated with recurrence. However, moderate sensitivity and false-negative results preclude treatment de-escalation based solely on negativity. In endometrial cancer, perioperative ctDNA has prognostic support from an 11-study, 1298-patient meta-analysis and additional cohorts, though assay heterogeneity and limited independent replication constrain clinical readiness. Postoperative ctDNA positivity generally demonstrates stronger associations with outcomes than preoperative detection.
Controversies and gaps
Assay heterogeneity and limited independent replication for ctDNA in endometrial cancer constrain clinical readiness. For cervical cancer, moderate sensitivity and false-negative results in ctDNA detection limit its utility for treatment de-escalation. Cervicovaginal and urine DNA-methylation studies provide preliminary evidence for detecting recurrence but lack prospective molecular lead time or clinical utility. Circulating tumor cells, extracellular vesicles, microRNAs, tumor-educated platelets, and fragmentomics remain exploratory with no established clinical validity.
Research and practice implications
Liquid biopsy should remain an investigational adjunct until prospective trials demonstrate that acting on molecular findings improves patient outcomes. Future research should prioritize standardized, disease-specific assays with validated thresholds for clinical decision-making, particularly for HPV-associated cervical and endometrial cancers. Prospective studies are needed to establish the clinical utility of ctDNA and other biomarkers in guiding treatment adjustments and surveillance strategies.

C 补充信息学术文献 · 综述C supplementaryAcademic literature · Review

细胞解析病毒-宿主 程序跨越与五种人类肿瘤病毒相关的癌症:系统评价

Cell-resolved virus-host programs across cancers associated with five human oncoviruses: a systematic review
作者： Rocha WMd, Willker Menezes da Rocha
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述使用单细胞 RNA 测序来解析病毒 宿主 转录程序,检查 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 相关癌症,重点关注 HPV 类型(例如 HPV16、HPV18)和 HPV 相关疾病,如宫颈上皮内瘤变和 HPV 相关宫颈癌、肛门癌和口咽癌。核心问题涉及 HPV 阳性肿瘤中细胞解析的转录变化与肿瘤组成的变化有何不同,以及这些癌症中出现的复发性 宿主 基因功能。
证据基础与方法
该综述是对 2020 年至 2026 年 8 月 2 日发表的人类单细胞 RNA 测序研究的系统综合,通过 PubMed/MEDLINE 进行鉴定,并在 Scopus 和 Web of Science 中进行补充检查。资格标准包括针对 HPV、EB 病毒 (EBV)、乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV)、人类 T 细胞白血病病毒 1 型 (HTLV-1) 或默克尔细胞多瘤病毒 (MCPyV) 的研究,其中 75 份报告符合纳入标准(HPV:34 份报告)。
共识与结论
在 HPV 相关癌症中,复发性 宿主 基因功能包括干扰素反应、抗原呈递、克隆淋巴细胞扩增和 B 细胞或三级淋巴组织,以及检查点、T 细胞耗竭、调节性 T 细胞、抑制性骨髓和基质状态以及抗原呈递丧失。在恶性 HPV 阳性细胞内,病毒转录本丰度与分化、整合、增殖、侵袭或 治疗 反应存在不同的相关性,具体取决于病毒类型、谱系、组织、疾病阶段和治疗。
争议与缺口
研究结果并未集中在 HPV 相关癌症的单一 宿主 基因特征上,这表明转录程序存在异质性。该综述强调,缺乏细胞水平病毒读取分配、病原学匹配对照的患者水平分析、空间或纵向采样以及正交验证的研究信息量较少,并且可能导致不一致的结论。
科研与实践启示
为了从 HPV 相关癌症的细胞分辨病毒 宿主 程序中产生可重复的生物标志物,未来的研究应将细胞水平病毒读取分配与患者水平分析相结合,包括病原学匹配的对照,采用空间或纵向采样,并纳入正交验证。诸如此类的设计特征对于克服当前的异质性和提高生物标志物的可靠性是必要的。

Cell-resolved virus-host programs across cancers associated with five human oncoviruses: a systematic review

Authors: Rocha WMd, Willker Menezes da Rocha
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Single-cell RNA sequencing can separate transcriptional changes within a cell type from shifts in tumor composition, yet findings from virus-associated cancers remain scattered across diseases and study designs. Human single-cell RNA-sequencing studies were reviewed, including cancers associated with human papillomavirus (HPV), Epstein-Barr virus (EBV), hepatitis B virus (HBV), human T-cell leukemia virus type 1 (HTLV-1), or Merkel cell polyomavirus (MCPyV). PubMed/MEDLINE was searched for reports published from 2020 to 2 August 2026, with complementary coverage checks in Scopus and Web of Science. Seventy-five reports met the eligibility criteria (HPV, 34; EBV, 14; HBV, 18; HTLV-1, 5; MCPyV, 4). Findings did not converge on a single host-gene signature. Instead, several functions recurred in different cellular compartments: interferon responses, antigen presentation, clonal lymphocyte expansion, and B-cell or tertiary-lymphoid organization were accompanied by checkpoints, T-cell exhaustion, regulatory T cells, suppressive myeloid and stromal states, and loss of antigen presentation. Within malignant cells, viral transcript abundance was associated with differentiation, integration, proliferation, invasion, or treatment response, although these relationships varied by virus, lineage, tissue, disease stage, and therapy. More informative studies combined cell-level viral-read assignment with patient-level analysis, etiologically matched controls, spatial or longitudinal sampling, and orthogonal validation. These design features will be necessary if cell-resolved virus-host programs are to yield reproducible biomarkers.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines human papillomavirus (HPV)-associated cancers using single-cell RNA sequencing to resolve virus-host transcriptional programs, focusing on HPV types (e.g., HPV16, HPV18) and HPV-associated diseases such as cervical intraepithelial neoplasia and HPV-associated cervical, anal, and oropharyngeal cancers. The central question addresses how cell-resolved transcriptional changes in HPV-positive tumors differ from shifts in tumor composition and what recurrent host-gene functions emerge across these cancers.
Evidence base and method
The review is a systematic synthesis of human single-cell RNA-sequencing studies published from 2020 to 2 August 2026, identified via PubMed/MEDLINE with complementary checks in Scopus and Web of Science. Eligibility criteria included studies on HPV, Epstein-Barr virus (EBV), hepatitis B virus (HBV), human T-cell leukemia virus type 1 (HTLV-1), or Merkel cell polyomavirus (MCPyV), with 75 reports meeting inclusion criteria (HPV: 34 reports).
Consensus and conclusions
Across HPV-associated cancers, recurrent host-gene functions include interferon responses, antigen presentation, clonal lymphocyte expansion, and B-cell or tertiary-lymphoid organization, alongside checkpoints, T-cell exhaustion, regulatory T cells, suppressive myeloid and stromal states, and loss of antigen presentation. Within malignant HPV-positive cells, viral transcript abundance correlates variably with differentiation, integration, proliferation, invasion, or treatment response depending on virus type, lineage, tissue, disease stage, and therapy.
Controversies and gaps
Findings did not converge on a single host-gene signature across HPV-associated cancers, indicating heterogeneity in transcriptional programs. The review highlights that studies lacking cell-level viral-read assignment, patient-level analysis with etiologically matched controls, spatial or longitudinal sampling, and orthogonal validation are less informative and may contribute to inconsistent conclusions.
Research and practice implications
To yield reproducible biomarkers from cell-resolved virus-host programs in HPV-associated cancers, future studies should integrate cell-level viral-read assignment with patient-level analysis, include etiologically matched controls, employ spatial or longitudinal sampling, and incorporate orthogonal validation. Design features such as these are necessary to overcome current heterogeneity and improve biomarker reliability.

伴有生殖道播散的宫颈混合性小细胞神经内分泌癌和腺癌:病例报告 和文献综述

Cervical mixed small cell neuroendocrine carcinoma and adenocarcinoma with genital tract dissemination: a case report and literature review
作者： Ping Liu, Hong Fang, Xiaolin Tang, Qing-xu Dong, Taohong Xie, Junjian Feng
摘要中文翻译背景 宫颈原发性混合神经内分泌-非神经内分泌肿瘤 (MiNEN) 极为罕见。本报告描述了一例 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 相关宫颈 MiNEN,由宫颈腺癌和小细胞神经内分泌癌 (SCNEC) 组成。临床和病理结果表明,腺癌成分通过生殖道播散延伸至子宫内膜、双侧输卵管和卵巢。病例介绍 一名 48 岁女性,阴道分泌物异常,持续 8 个多月,HPV 基因分型检测呈 18 型阳性。盆腔 MRI 显示宫颈占位性病变和附件区囊性变。肉眼检查发现溃疡性宫颈肿块、输卵管增厚和扩张以及多囊卵巢。瘤体实性,切面灰白色,有粘液。组织学上,宫颈肿瘤包含 65% SCNEC 和 35% HPV 相关腺癌,两者均广泛表达 p16。子宫内膜、双侧输卵管和卵巢均发现腺癌,其形态、免疫表型和HPV感染状况与宫颈腺癌一致,提示宫颈腺癌沿生殖道播散。尽管术后接受了全身化疗和局部放疗,但患者还是因病情进展于 2022 年 11 月不幸去世。结论 宫颈 MiNEN 是一种高度侵袭性恶性肿瘤,预后不良。HPV 相关肿瘤可能通过生殖道连续扩散,类似于子宫内膜、输卵管或卵巢的原发肿瘤。准确的诊断和对患者的有效管理依赖于细致的术后组织病理学评估。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注宫颈混合性小细胞神经内分泌癌和伴有生殖道播散的腺癌,特别研究了 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 在这些恶性肿瘤中的作用。核心问题是 HPV 相关肿瘤是否可以通过生殖道连续扩散,模仿子宫内膜、输卵管或卵巢的原发性肿瘤。
证据基础与方法
该综述是 病例报告 和文献综述。它没有明确报告数据库、检索日期、资格标准或荟萃分析。证据来自单个案例和文献的一般观察。
共识与结论
宫颈 MiNEN 是一种高度侵袭性的恶性肿瘤,预后不良。它还强调,HPV 相关肿瘤可能通过生殖道连续扩散,可能模仿其他生殖道部位的原发性肿瘤。
争议与缺口
该综述指出,HPV 相关肿瘤通过生殖道的传播尚不清楚,并且关于传播机制的证据有限。所描述的案例是独特的,需要进一步的研究来在更广泛的背景下证实这些发现。
科研与实践启示
该综述强调了准确的 诊断 和对颈部 MiNEN 患者有效管理的重要性,这需要细致的术后组织病理学评估。它还表明需要对生殖道中 HPV 相关肿瘤的行为和传播进行更多研究。

Cervical mixed small cell neuroendocrine carcinoma and adenocarcinoma with genital tract dissemination: a case report and literature review

Authors: Ping Liu, Hong Fang, Xiaolin Tang, Qing-xu Dong, Taohong Xie, Junjian Feng
Original abstract (English)
Background Primary mixed neuroendocrine-non-neuroendocrine neoplasms (MiNENs) of the cervix are exceedingly rare. This report describes a case of human papillomavirus (HPV)-associated cervical MiNEN composed of adenocarcinoma and small cell neuroendocrine carcinoma (SCNEC) of cervix. Clinical and pathological findings suggest that the adenocarcinoma component extended to the endometrium, bilateral fallopian tubes, and ovaries via genital tract dissemination. Case presentation A 48-year-old female presented with abnormal vaginal discharge for more than eight months and HPV genotyping test was positive for type 18. Pelvic MRI revealed cervical space-occupying lesions and cystic changes in the adnexal region. Gross examination revealed an ulcerative cervical mass, thickened and dilated fallopian tubes, and polycystic ovaries. The tumor was solid, and the cut surface was grayish white with mucus. Histologically, the cervical tumor comprised 65% SCNEC and 35% HPV-associated adenocarcinoma, both of which diffusely expressed p16. Adenocarcinoma was identified in the endometrium and bilateral fallopian tubes and ovaries, and its morphology, immunophenotype, and HPV infection status were consistent with those of cervical adenocarcinoma, suggesting that the cervical adenocarcinoma disseminated along the reproductive tract. Despite receiving systemic chemotherapy and local radiotherapy following surgery, the patient unfortunately passed away due to the progression of the disease in November 2022. Conclusions Cervical MiNENs are highly aggressive malignancies associated with poor prognoses. HPV-associated tumors may spread contiguously through the reproductive tract, mimicking primary tumors of the endometrium, fallopian tubes, or ovaries. Accurate diagnosis and effective management of patients rely on meticulous postoperative histopathological evaluation.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on cervical mixed small cell neuroendocrine carcinoma and adenocarcinoma with genital tract dissemination, specifically examining the role of human papillomavirus (HPV) in these malignancies. The central question is whether HPV-associated tumors can spread contiguously through the reproductive tract, mimicking primary tumors of the endometrium, fallopian tubes, or ovaries.
Evidence base and method
The review is a case report and literature review. It does not explicitly report on databases, search dates, eligibility criteria, or meta-analysis. The evidence is derived from a single case and general observations from the literature.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that cervical MiNENs are highly aggressive malignancies associated with poor prognoses. It also highlights that HPV-associated tumors may spread contiguously through the reproductive tract, potentially mimicking primary tumors in other genital tract sites.
Controversies and gaps
The review notes that the spread of HPV-associated tumors through the genital tract is not well understood, and there is limited evidence on the mechanisms of dissemination. The case described is unique, and further studies are needed to confirm these findings in a broader context.
Research and practice implications
The review emphasizes the importance of accurate diagnosis and effective management of patients with cervical MiNENs, which requires meticulous postoperative histopathological evaluation. It also suggests the need for more research on the behavior and spread of HPV-associated tumors in the genital tract.

阴道微生物组在人乳头瘤病毒持续感染及宫颈上皮内瘤变进展为宫颈癌中的作用

Academic paper (Cyrillic-language original; verified English title unavailable)
作者： А. Ю. Павлючук, М. И. Шопик
摘要中文翻译В обзоре представлен анализ современных данных о взаимосвязи состава вагинального микробиома с течением папилломавирусной инфекции у женщин в Республике Беларусь。Приведены данные о молекулярно-эпидемиологических особенностях циркуляции вируса папилломы человека в белорусской популяции и перспективах использования пробиотических штаммов (卷曲乳杆菌) в национальных клинических протоколах。该综述对白俄罗斯共和国女性阴道微生物组成与 人乳头瘤病毒 感染病程之间关系的当前数据进行了分析。特别关注乳酸杆菌的物种多样性及其在清除或持续高风险 人乳头瘤病毒 中的作用。考虑了在菌群失调背景下宫颈上皮内瘤变进展为 宫颈癌 的机制。介绍了白俄罗斯人群中 人乳头瘤病毒 循环的分子和流行病学特征的数据以及在国家临床方案中使用益生菌菌株(卷曲乳杆菌)的前景。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述探讨了女性阴道微生物组的组成与 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 感染病程之间的关系,重点关注白俄罗斯共和国 HPV 相关的宫颈上皮内瘤变和 宫颈癌 的进展。核心问题是阴道微生物群,特别是乳酸菌,如何影响 HPV 的持续存在、清除和疾病进展。
证据基础与方法
该综述综合了白俄罗斯人群中 HPV 分子流行病学特征的最新数据,并评估了益生菌菌株(特别是卷曲乳杆菌)在国家临床方案中的作用。它整合了乳酸杆菌的物种多样性及其与 高危型HPV 清除或持久性的关联,以及生态失调背景下宫颈上皮内瘤变进展为癌症的机制。
共识与结论
该综述的结论是,乳杆菌物种多样性,特别是卷曲乳杆菌,与 高危型HPV 的清除或持久性相关。它强调了宫颈上皮内瘤变进展为 宫颈癌 与阴道菌群失调状态有关,强调了益生菌干预在国家临床方案中的潜在作用。
争议与缺口
该综述没有报告益生菌菌株在 HPV 清除中的有效性或生态失调导致 宫颈癌 进展的机制的具体定量数据。此外,它缺乏对纳入研究或汇总估计的详细方法描述来证实这些关联。
科研与实践启示
该审查建议优先研究将卷曲乳杆菌等益生菌菌株纳入国家 HPV 管理临床方案。它还意味着需要进一步研究将阴道生态失调与 HPV 持续性和 宫颈癌 进展联系起来的分子机制,特别是在白俄罗斯人群中。

Academic paper (Cyrillic-language original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Cyrillic-language)
РОЛЬ ВАГИНАЛЬНОГО МИКРОБИОМА В ПЕРСИСТЕНЦИИ ВИРУСА ПАПИЛЛОМЫ ЧЕЛОВЕКА И ПРОГРЕССИИ ЦЕРВИКАЛЬНОЙ ИНТРАЭПИТЕЛИАЛЬНОЙ НЕОПЛАЗИИ В РАК ШЕЙКИ МАТКИ
Authors: А. Ю. Павлючук, М. И. Шопик
Original abstract (English)
В обзоре представлен анализ современных данных о взаимосвязи состава вагинального микробиома с течением папилломавирусной инфекции у женщин в Республике Беларусь. Приведены данные о молекулярно-эпидемиологических особенностях циркуляции вируса папилломы человека в белорусской популяции и перспективах использования пробиотических штаммов (Lactobacillus crispatus) в национальных клинических протоколах. The review presents an analysis of current data on the relationship between the composition of the vaginal microbiome and the course of human papillomavirus infection in women in the Republic of Belarus. Special attention is paid to the species diversity of lactobacilli and their role in the clearance or persistence of high-risk human papillomavirus. The mechanisms of progression of cervical intraepithelial neoplasia to cervical cancer against the background of dysbiotic disorders are considered. Data on the molecular and epidemiological features of human papillomavirus circulation in the Belarusian population and the prospects for using probiotic strains (Lactobacillus crispatus) in national clinical protocols are presented.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines the relationship between the composition of the vaginal microbiome and the course of human papillomavirus (HPV) infection in women, focusing on HPV-associated cervical intraepithelial neoplasia and progression to cervical cancer in the Republic of Belarus. The central question addresses how vaginal microbiota, particularly Lactobacillus species, influence HPV persistence, clearance, and disease progression.
Evidence base and method
The review synthesizes current data on HPV molecular-epidemiological features in the Belarusian population and evaluates the role of probiotic strains, specifically Lactobacillus crispatus, in national clinical protocols. It integrates species diversity of lactobacilli and their association with high-risk HPV clearance or persistence, as well as mechanisms underlying cervical intraepithelial neoplasia progression to cancer in the context of dysbiosis.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that Lactobacillus species diversity, particularly Lactobacillus crispatus, is associated with the clearance or persistence of high-risk HPV. It highlights the progression of cervical intraepithelial neoplasia to cervical cancer as linked to dysbiotic vaginal microbiome states, emphasizing the potential role of probiotic interventions in national clinical protocols.
Controversies and gaps
The review does not report specific quantitative data on the effectiveness of probiotic strains in HPV clearance or the mechanisms by which dysbiosis contributes to cervical cancer progression. Additionally, it lacks detailed methodological descriptions of included studies or pooled estimates to substantiate these associations.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests prioritizing research on the integration of probiotic strains, such as Lactobacillus crispatus, into national clinical protocols for HPV management. It also implies the need for further investigation into the molecular mechanisms linking vaginal dysbiosis to HPV persistence and cervical cancer progression, particularly in the Belarusian population.

🚨 突发动态与新闻 / Health News

Published: 2026-08-10  |  Publisher: IARC – INTERNATIONAL AGENCY FOR RESEARCH ON CANCER
A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

IARC 及其合作伙伴授予赠款以改善冲突地区的 宫颈癌 预防 - IARC – 国际癌症研究机构

IARC and partners awarded grant to improve cervical cancer prevention in conflict zones - IARC – INTERNATIONAL AGENCY FOR RESEARCH ON CANCER
新闻简报国际癌症研究机构 (IARC) 及其合作机构已获得一笔赠款,用于加强冲突地区的 宫颈癌 预防工作。该倡议是 IARC 更广泛使命的一部分,旨在促进全球癌症预防并解决医疗保健获取方面的差异。这笔赠款旨在改善医疗基础设施经常受到损害、导致对该疾病的认识和信息有限的地区的 宫颈癌 预防。虽然证据中没有详细说明赠款影响的确切范围和规模,但重点是处于危险之中的人群,特别是育龄妇女。该活动已被确认为官方资助奖项,并且没有提及任何不确定性或未解决的信息。该倡议反映了国际癌症研究机构致力于通过有针对性的研究和能力建设努力应对弱势社区与癌症相关的挑战。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该新闻报道于 2026 年 8 月 10 日发布。该活动与 IARC 及其合作伙伴授予的一项赠款有关,该赠款旨在改善冲突地区的 宫颈癌 预防工作,该赠款正在进行中。
地点与对象
该活动涉及全球冲突地区,重点关注受 宫颈癌 影响的人群,特别是育龄妇女。
事件
IARC 及其合作伙伴获得了一笔赠款,用于改善冲突地区的 宫颈癌 预防工作。这是 IARC 的正式公告,该活动已确认为赠款奖项。
规模、影响与风险
该赠款旨在解决冲突地区的 宫颈癌 预防问题,这些地区的医疗保健服务有限。影响的规模没有在证据中量化,但通过冲突地区和缺乏信息的背景,人们隐含地承认了这种风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
该赠款是 IARC 及其合作伙伴的官方行动。响应状态已确认,证据中没有表明不确定或未解决的信息。
A high priorityPublic-health news

IARC and partners awarded grant to improve cervical cancer prevention in conflict zones - IARC – INTERNATIONAL AGENCY FOR RESEARCH ON CANCER

News Brief
The International Agency for Research on Cancer (IARC) and its partner institutions have been awarded a grant to enhance cervical cancer prevention efforts in conflict zones. This initiative is part of IARC's broader mission to promote global cancer prevention and address disparities in healthcare access. The grant is aimed at improving cervical cancer prevention in regions where healthcare infrastructure is often compromised, leading to limited awareness and information about the disease. While the exact scope and scale of the grant's impact are not detailed in the evidence, the focus is on populations at risk, particularly women of reproductive age. The event is confirmed as an official grant award, and no uncertainty or unresolved information is mentioned. The initiative reflects IARC's commitment to tackling cancer-related challenges in vulnerable communities through targeted research and capacity-building efforts.
Original source text (English)

巴布亚新几内亚采取重大措施保护女孩和妇女免受 宫颈癌 感染 - 世界卫生组织 (WHO)

Papua New Guinea takes a major step to protect girls and women from cervical cancer - World Health Organization (WHO)
新闻简报巴布亚新几内亚收到了第一批 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 疫苗,在保护女孩和妇女免受 宫颈癌 侵害方面迈出了重要一步。疫苗 是全国免疫计划的一部分,旨在预防 宫颈癌,这是该国女性癌症死亡的主要原因。该计划的第一阶段针对约 60,000 名 9 至 14 岁的女孩,预计将于 2026 年 9 月 10 日开始。预计未来几个月将提供更多 疫苗 物资,计划分发总计 280,000 瓶。该举措体现了政府强有力的领导力以及与世界卫生组织 (WHO)、联合国儿童基金会和全球疫苗和免疫联盟等全球卫生合作伙伴的合作,以确保获得安全有效的 宫颈癌 保护。这项努力是减轻 HPV 相关 宫颈癌 负担并通过预防拯救生命的更广泛战略的一部分。
查看新闻五要素
时间
第一批 HPV 疫苗 抵达巴布亚新几内亚,该计划的第一阶段预计于 2026 年 9 月 10 日开始。更多的 疫苗 供应预计将在未来几个月内抵达。
地点与对象
巴布亚新几内亚在全国推广的第一阶段针对约 60,000 名 9 至 14 岁的女孩,并计划扩展到更多省份。
事件
随着第一批 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 疫苗 的抵达,巴布亚新几内亚在对抗 宫颈癌 的斗争中达到了一个重要的里程碑。将 HPV 疫苗 纳入国家免疫计划代表着对未来几代女性健康的重大投资。
规模、影响与风险
该国最近收到了 100,000 瓶 疫苗,预计还会有 180,000 瓶到达,使总供应量达到 280,000 瓶。宫颈癌 仍然是该国的一个重大公共卫生挑战,数百名妇女死于一种基本上可以预防的疾病。
应对、状态与不确定性
国家卫生部与国家教育部和省卫生当局合作,领导该计划的规划和实施。世界卫生组织(WHO)正在为政策制定、规划规划、卫生工作者培训以及监测和评估提供技术支持。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Papua New Guinea takes a major step to protect girls and women from cervical cancer - World Health Organization (WHO)

News Brief
Papua New Guinea has taken a major step in protecting girls and women from cervical cancer by receiving its first shipment of human papillomavirus (HPV) vaccines. The vaccines, which are part of a nationwide immunization programme, aim to prevent cervical cancer, a leading cause of cancer deaths among women in the country. The first phase of the programme, targeting approximately 60,000 girls aged 9 to 14 years, is expected to begin on 10 September 2026. Additional vaccine supplies are anticipated in the coming months, with a total of 280,000 vials planned for distribution. The initiative reflects strong government leadership and collaboration with global health partners, including the World Health Organization (WHO), UNICEF, and Gavi, to ensure access to safe and effective protection against cervical cancer. This effort is part of a broader strategy to reduce the burden of HPV-associated cervical cancer and save lives through prevention.
Original source text (English)

Western Pacific Papua New Guinea Global Regions WHO Regional websites Africa Americas South-East Asia Europe Eastern Mediterranean Western Pacific Countries Countries, territories and areas in the WHO Western Pacific Region: American Samoa (USA) * Australia Brunei Darussalam Cambodia China Cook Islands Fiji French Polynesia (France) Guam (USA) * Hong Kong SAR (China) Indonesia Japan Kiribati Lao People's Democratic Republic Macao SAR (China) Malaysia Marshall Islands Micronesia, Federated States of Mongolia Naoero New Caledonia (France) New Zealand Niue Northern Mariana Islands (USA) * Palau Papua New Guinea Philippines Pitcairn Islands (UK) Republic of Korea Samoa Singapore Solomon Islands Tokelau Tonga Tuvalu Vanuatu Viet Nam Wallis and Futuna (France) Home Health topics Our work News Emergencies About us Home Health topics Our work News Emergencies About us Home/ News/ Detail/ Papua New Guinea takes a major step to protect girls and women from cervical cancer © WHO / Glenda Gonzales The first shipment of HPV vaccines for Papua New Guinea, marking the start of a nationwide effort to protect girls from cervical cancer. © Credits Papua New Guinea takes a major step to protect girls and women from cervical cancer 11 August 2026 News release Papua New Guinea Reading time: 2 min (535 words) Papua New Guinea has reached an important milestone in the fight against cervical cancer with the arrival of its first shipment of human papillomavirus (HPV) vaccines. The country recently received 100 000 vaccine vials, marking the beginning of a nationwide effort to protect girls from one of the leading causes of cancer deaths among women in Papua New Guinea. A further 180 000 vaccine vials are expected to arrive in the coming months, bringing the total supply to 280 000 vials. The introduction of the HPV vaccine into Papua New Guinea's national immunization programme represents a significant investment in the health and well-being of future generations of women. Cervical cancer remains a major public health challenge in the country, with hundreds of women losing their lives to a disease that is largely preventable. Vaccination offers one of the most effective ways to reduce this burden and save lives. The HPV is a common virus that can cause cervical cancer. While many HPV infections resolve naturally, some types of the virus can lead to changes in cervical cells that may develop into cancer over time. The HPV vaccine protects girls before they are exposed to the virus, helping prevent cervical cancer later in life. The vaccine has a strong global safety record and plays a critical role in the global effort to eliminate cervical cancer. HPV vaccines. © WHO / Glenda Gonzales The first phase of the programme is expected to begin on 10 September 2026 and will target approximately 60 000 girls aged 9 to 14 years during the first phase of the national rollout. As additional vaccine supplies arrive and implementation capacity increases, the programme will progressively expand to more provinces, helping ensure girls across the country can access this life-saving protection. This achievement reflects strong leadership from the Government of Papua New Guinea, which has allocated PGK 6.2 million in the 2026 National Budget to support the introduction of the HPV vaccine. The National Department of Health, working closely with the National Department of Education and Provincial Health Authorities, is leading the planning and delivery of the programme. The rollout is also the result of a strong partnership between the Government of Papua New Guinea and global health partners. The World Health Organization (WHO) is providing technical support for policy development, programme planning, health worker training, and monitoring and evaluation. UNICEF is supporting vaccine procurement and delivery, cold chain and supply systems, programme planning and engagement with families and communities. Gavi, the Vaccine Alliance, is providing vital co-financing support to help make the programme possible. © WHO Papua New Guinea Together, these partners are working to ensure that girls across Papua New Guinea have access to safe, effective protection against cervical cancer. Their collaboration demonstrates what can be achieved when governments and development partners work together towards a shared public health goal. The introduction of HPV vaccination is an important step towards a future where fewer women develop cervical cancer and fewer families experience the loss of mothers, daughters, sisters and loved ones to a preventable disease. By investing in prevention today, Papua New Guinea is helping protect the health of future generations and moving closer to a world free from cervical cancer
View five news elements
Time
The first shipment of HPV vaccines arrived in Papua New Guinea, with the first phase of the programme expected to begin on 10 September 2026. Further vaccine supplies are expected to arrive in the coming months.
Location and population
Papua New Guinea, targeting approximately 60,000 girls aged 9 to 14 years in the first phase of the national rollout, with plans to expand to more provinces.
Event
Papua New Guinea has reached an important milestone in the fight against cervical cancer with the arrival of its first shipment of human papillomavirus (HPV) vaccines. The introduction of the HPV vaccine into the national immunization programme represents a significant investment in the health of future generations of women.
Scale, impact and risk
The country recently received 100,000 vaccine vials, with an additional 180,000 expected to arrive, bringing the total supply to 280,000 vials. Cervical cancer remains a major public health challenge in the country, with hundreds of women losing their lives to a largely preventable disease.
Response, status and uncertainty
The National Department of Health, working with the National Department of Education and Provincial Health Authorities, is leading the planning and delivery of the programme. The World Health Organization (WHO) is providing technical support for policy development, programme planning, health worker training, and monitoring and evaluation.

MD 大学 Rohtak 研究在 宫颈癌 患者中标记了 HPV-16,18,并强调早期筛查 - 论坛报

MD University Rohtak study flags HPV-16,18 in cervical cancer patients, stresses early screening - The Tribune
新闻简报Rohtak 马里兰大学遗传学系进行的一项研究强调了 高危型HPV 16 型和 18 型在 宫颈癌 中的重要作用。这项由 Shalu 领导、Ritu Yadav 教授和 Meenakshi Chauhan 教授共同监督的研究发现,大多数 宫颈癌 患者体内都存在这些 HPV 类型。该研究还检查了长非编码 RNA 的分子变化,并确定了可以作为早期 诊断 和预后的生物标志物的遗传和表观遗传改变。这些发现可能会带来个性化的 治疗 和新的治疗方法。此外,研究人员强调了早期筛查和宣传计划的重要性,这些计划已经使农村和城市地区的 2,000 多名女性对 宫颈癌 的症状、危险因素和预防有了认识。Ritu Yadav 教授一直在印度北部积极提高人们对 宫颈癌 的认识,强调 HPV 疫苗接种、定期筛查和公共教育的必要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2026-08-12进行并报告。
地点与对象
该研究在印度罗塔克马里兰大学进行,重点关注 宫颈癌 患者。
事件
Rohtak MD 大学的一项研究确定了 高危型HPV 16 型和 18 型与 宫颈癌 相关,强调了早期筛查和检测的重要性。
规模、影响与风险
该研究发现,大多数 宫颈癌 患者存在 高危型HPV 16 型和 18 型,凸显了它们在 宫颈癌 风险中的重要作用。它还检查了长非编码 RNA 的分子变化,并确定了可以作为早期 诊断 和预后的生物标志物的遗传和表观遗传改变。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究的结果可能有助于为个性化 治疗 和更新的治疗方法铺平道路。Ritu Yadav 教授一直在印度北部地区提高人们对 宫颈癌 的认识,通过提高认识项目,超过 2,000 名女性已经了解了症状、风险因素、预防以及及时筛查的重要性。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

MD University Rohtak study flags HPV-16,18 in cervical cancer patients, stresses early screening - The Tribune

News Brief
A study conducted by the Department of Genetics at MD University, Rohtak, has highlighted the significant role of high-risk HPV types 16 and 18 in cervical cancer. The research, led by Shalu under the supervision of Prof Ritu Yadav and co-supervised by Prof Meenakshi Chauhan, found that these HPV types were present in a majority of cervical cancer patients. The study also examined molecular changes in long non-coding RNAs and identified genetic and epigenetic alterations that could serve as biomarkers for early diagnosis and prognosis. These findings could potentially lead to personalized treatment and new therapeutic approaches. Additionally, the researchers emphasized the importance of early screening and awareness programs, which have already sensitized over 2,000 women in rural and urban areas to the symptoms, risk factors, and prevention of cervical cancer. Prof Ritu Yadav has been actively promoting awareness about cervical cancer across North India, underscoring the need for HPV vaccination, regular screening, and public education.
Original source text (English)
MD University Rohtak study flags HPV-16,18 in cervical cancer patients, stresses early screening Researchers also identified factors like age at first childbirth, number of births, miscarriages and lifestyle-related factors as being associated with cervical cancer risk A research study by the Department of Genetics at Maharshi Dayanand University (MDU), Rohtak, has underlined the crucial role of HPV screening and early detection in the fight against cervical cancer, with high-risk HPV types 16 and 18 found in a majority of the patients studied. The study was conducted by research scholar Shalu under the supervision of Prof Ritu Yadav, Department of Genetics, MDU, with Prof Meenakshi Chauhan of the Department of Obstetrics and Gynaecology, PGIMS, Rohtak, as co-supervisor. The researchers also identified factors such as age at first childbirth, number of births, miscarriages and lifestyle-related factors as being associated with cervical cancer risk, said Pankaj Nain, Public Relations Officer, MDU. He maintained that the study further examined molecular changes in long non-coding RNAs and identified certain genetic and epigenetic alterations that could potentially serve as biomarkers for early diagnosis and prognosis. The findings could help pave the way for personalised treatment and newer therapeutic approaches. “Prof Ritu Yadav has been actively promoting awareness about cervical cancer across North India. Through awareness programmes, more than 2,000 women from rural and urban areas have so far been sensitised to the symptoms, risk factors, prevention and importance of timely screening. She stressed that HPV vaccination, regular screening and awareness are key to reducing the burden of cervical cancer,” the PRO added

Time
The study was conducted and reported on 2026-08-12.
Location and population
The study was conducted at MD University, Rohtak, India, focusing on cervical cancer patients.
Event
A research study by MD University, Rohtak, identified high-risk HPV types 16 and 18 as being associated with cervical cancer, emphasizing the importance of early screening and detection.
Scale, impact and risk
The study found high-risk HPV types 16 and 18 in a majority of cervical cancer patients, highlighting their significant role in cervical cancer risk. It also examined molecular changes in long non-coding RNAs and identified genetic and epigenetic alterations that could serve as biomarkers for early diagnosis and prognosis.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study's findings could help pave the way for personalized treatment and newer therapeutic approaches. Prof Ritu Yadav has been promoting awareness about cervical cancer across North India, and through awareness programs, more than 2,000 women have been sensitized to the symptoms, risk factors, prevention, and importance of timely screening.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

万泰生物:子公司九价HPV疫苗获泰国上市许可 - 东方财富

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报万泰生物旗下厦门万泰沧海生物科技有限公司宣布,其自主研发的9价人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)疫苗已获得泰国食品药品监督管理局的上市许可。该批准代表该产品获得第一个国际营销许可,使其能够出口到泰国市场。疫苗 专为 9 至 45 岁的女性设计,提供七种 高危型HPV 类型(16、18、31、33、45、52 和 58)和两种低风险类型(6 和 11)的保险,以预防相关疾病。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 8 月 11 日报道;事件在发布之前“最近”发生。
地点与对象
泰国;目标人群包括9-45岁女性。
事件
厦门万泰沧海生物科技有限公司(万泰生物子公司)自主研发的9价人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)疫苗(大肠杆菌)已获得泰国食品药品监督管理局上市许可。
规模、影响与风险
疫苗 涵盖 高危型HPV 七种类型(16、18、31、33、45、52、58)和两种低风险类型(6、11),以预防相关疾病。这标志着该产品首次在中国境外获得上市许可。
应对、状态与不确定性
疫苗已获准在泰国市场进口和销售。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
万泰生物:子公司九价HPV疫苗获泰国上市许可 - 东方财富
News Brief
Xiamen Wantai Canghai Biotechnology Co., Ltd., a subsidiary of Wantai Bio, has announced that its self-developed 9-valent Human Papillomavirus (HPV) vaccine has received marketing authorization from the Thai Food and Drug Administration. This approval represents the product's first international marketing license, enabling its export to the Thai market. The vaccine is designed for females aged 9 to 45 and provides coverage against seven high-risk HPV types (16, 18, 31, 33, 45, 52, and 58) and two low-risk types (6 and 11) to prevent associated diseases.

View five news elements
Time
Reported on August 11, 2026; event occurred 'ecently' prior to publication.
Location and population
Thailand; target population includes females aged 9-45 years.
Event
Xiamen Wantai Canghai Biotechnology Co., Ltd. (a subsidiary of Wantai Bio) has received marketing authorization from the Thai Food and Drug Administration for its self-developed 9-valent Human Papillomavirus (HPV) vaccine (Escherichia coli).
Scale, impact and risk
The vaccine covers seven high-risk HPV types (16, 18, 31, 33, 45, 52, 58) and two low-risk types (6, 11) to prevent related diseases. This marks the product's first marketing authorization outside of China.
Response, status and uncertainty
The vaccine has been approved for import and sale in the Thai market.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

HPV 自检扩大了 宫颈癌 筛查范围 - 华盛顿大学医学 |编辑部

HPV self-testing expands cervical cancer screening - UW Medicine | Newsroom
新闻简报华盛顿大学医学研究人员在阿片类药物 治疗 项目中引入了 HPV 自我检测,以扩大 宫颈癌 筛查,特别是在服务不足的人群中。该倡议于 2026 年 7 月发布,旨在提高通常无法使用传统方法的患者的筛查率。在每年的健康检查中,25 岁​​或以上的宫颈患者都会接受自我检测,事实证明,这种检测与临床医生进行的筛查一样准确。研究发现,与华盛顿最近的一项大型筛查研究相比,高危型HPV 的检测率几乎翻倍。早期检测 HPV 可以去除癌组织,而晚期 宫颈癌 通常需要放疗和化疗。尽管后续护理提出了挑战,但这个过程对工作人员和患者来说都很容易。该团队希望将这项服务扩展到更多为农场工人和无家可归者等边缘群体服务的阿片类药物 治疗 项目和诊所,以减少 宫颈癌 死亡率 的数量。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于 2026 年 7 月发表,在患者每年的健康就诊期间提供 HPV 自检,该研究由 NIH 的 NCI 资助。
地点与对象
该倡议是在 We Care Daily Clinics 实施的,这是一个阿片类药物 治疗 计划,针对阿片类药物使用障碍患者和其他筛查不足的人群,如农场工人和无家可归的人。
事件
华盛顿大学医学院的研究人员进行了一项研究,通过在阿片类药物 治疗 计划中提供 HPV 自检来扩大 宫颈癌 筛查,这提高了经常不进行筛查的患者的筛查率。
规模、影响与风险
与最近在华盛顿进行的一项大型 宫颈癌 筛查研究相比,自我测试方法显示 高危型HPV 的阳性率几乎翻倍。早期检测可以减少 死亡率,而后期 宫颈癌 通常需要放疗和化疗。
应对、状态与不确定性
研究发现,90%的患者应该接受筛查,90%的患者希望进行自我筛查。这个过程对于诊所工作人员和患者来说都很容易,但将他们与后续护理联系起来是一个挑战。希望该计划能够扩展到更多的阿片类药物 治疗 项目和诊所,为筛查不足的人群提供服务。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

HPV self-testing expands cervical cancer screening - UW Medicine | Newsroom

News Brief
UW Medicine researchers have introduced HPV self-testing at an opioid treatment program to expand cervical cancer screening, particularly among underserved populations. The initiative, published in July 2026, aimed to increase screening rates among patients who often lack access to traditional methods. During annual wellness visits, patients 25 or older with a cervix were offered self-testing, which proved to be as accurate as clinician-administered screening. The study found that nearly double the rate of high-risk HPV was detected compared to a recent large screening study in Washington. Early detection of HPV can lead to the removal of cancerous tissue, while later-stage cervical cancer typically requires radiation and chemotherapy. The process was easy for both staff and patients, though follow-up care posed a challenge. The team hopes to expand this service to more opioid-treatment programs and clinics serving marginalized groups, such as farmworkers and unhoused individuals, to reduce cervical cancer mortality.
Original source text (English)
HPV self-testing expands cervical cancer screening Earlier detection could reduce deaths from the disease in high-risk, underscreened populations. Media Contact: Vishva Nalamalapu - vnala@uw.edu Offering human papilloma virus (HPV) self-testing at an opioid treatment program increased cervical cancer screening among patients who often go without it, according to research published in July's JAMA Network Open. Expanded screening could help people detect cervical cancer at earlier stages and could reduce mortality as a result. “We have to get out into new environments to reach patients who are not being reached through standard testing,” said Elizabeth Swisher, a professor of gynecologic oncology at the University of Washington School of Medicine and the study’s lead author. Swisher cares for patients with advanced stage cervical cancer, which often stems from HPV infections. She has grown frustrated, she said, that a lack of access to early HPV screening results in many patients needing intense medical therapies and facing high mortality risk. People who are marginalized from the healthcare system, such as those with opioid use disorder, are more likely to be unscreened. People with opioid use disorder also have higher risk factors for cervical cancer. “This is a very underscreened patient population and a high-risk patient population,” said Swisher. Swisher’s team partnered with We Care Daily Clinics, an opioid-treatment program that also provides holistic care, to offer HPV self-testing to their patients. During patients’ annual wellness visits, Swisher's team offered testing to everyone 25 or older who had a cervix and was overdue for screening or unsure when they were last screened. Screening has traditionally been done through a pelvic exam, but the team gave patients a vaginal swab to self-screen. This approach is just as accurate as a clinician-administered screen, can be done in locations without gynecological services, and is more comfortable for many patients. When test results were positive for high-risk HPV strains, the clinic connected those patients with follow-up care ranging from a Pap smear, which collects cells from the cervix, to a colposcopy, which uses a magnifying instrument to look at the cervix. Most people with earlier stages of cervical cancer can get the cancerous tissue removed. For people whose disease is at a later stage, treatment usually involves radiation and chemotherapy. Swisher’s team found that 90% of patients were due for screening. Of those, 90% wanted to self-screen. The patients' swabs showed nearly double the rate of positive, high-risk HPV, compared to a recent large cervical cancer screening study in Washington. HPV self-testing was an easy process for both clinic staff and patients. Connecting patients with follow-up care was a challenge, but overall, offering self-testing in the clinic was an important first step to expand screening, Swisher said. “Screening the unscreened will make an impact on cervical cancer mortality,” she said. Her team is hoping this service will expand to more opioid-treatment programs and other clinics serving underscreened populations, such as farmworkers and unhoused people. “This could become a standard offering.” The research was funded by Cancer Center Support Grant P30 CA015704 from the NCI of the NIH. Related: HPV vaccine prevents cervical cancer before it starts For details about UW Medicine, please see our About page
View five news elements
Time
The study was published in July 2026, and the HPV self-testing was offered during patients' annual wellness visits, with the research funded by the NCI of the NIH.
Location and population
The initiative was implemented at We Care Daily Clinics, an opioid-treatment program, targeting patients with opioid use disorder and other underscreened populations such as farmworkers and unhoused individuals.
Event
UW Medicine researchers conducted a study to expand cervical cancer screening by offering HPV self-testing at an opioid treatment program, which increased screening rates among patients who often go without it.
Scale, impact and risk
The self-testing approach showed nearly double the rate of positive, high-risk HPV compared to a recent large cervical cancer screening study in Washington. Early detection can reduce mortality, while later-stage cervical cancer typically requires radiation and chemotherapy.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study found that 90% of patients were due for screening, and 90% wanted to self-screen. The process was easy for both clinic staff and patients, but connecting them with follow-up care was a challenge. The initiative is hoped to expand to more opioid-treatment programs and clinics serving underscreened populations.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

除了 宫颈癌:HPV 疫苗 实际上可以预防 5 种疾病 - theweek.in

Beyond cervical cancer: 5 diseases the HPV vaccine can actually prevent - theweek.in
新闻简报The article published on August 09, 2026, by Dr. Aruna Kalra highlights that the HPV vaccine offers protection against diseases beyond cervical cancer, including oropharyngeal cancers, anal cancer, vulvar and vaginal cancers, and genital warts. It emphasizes the importance of vaccinating adolescents before exposure to the virus, as there is no routine screening for throat cancer, making prevention through vaccination more critical. The piece also addresses the misconception that HPV vaccination is solely for women, noting that it is equally relevant for men. While the vaccine targets specific high-risk strains, it is effective in preventing a substantial proportion of these diseases. The article underscores the need for more accurate and inclusive public awareness about the vaccine's broader health implications.
查看新闻五要素
时间
The article was published on August 09, 2026, and discusses the HPV vaccine's benefits in preventing various diseases, including cancers and genital warts, with implications for vaccination timing and public awareness.
地点与对象
该文章本质上是一般性的,没有指定特定的地点或人群。它讨论了 HPV 疫苗 在预防所有性别和人群疾病方面的作用。
事件
文章解释说,HPV 疫苗 可预防 宫颈癌 以外的疾病,例如口咽癌、肛门癌、外阴癌和阴道癌以及生殖器疣。它强调了在接触病毒之前 疫苗接种 的重要性,并消除了对其与男性相关性的误解。
规模、影响与风险
文章指出,在一些国家,与 HPV 相关的口咽癌现在对男性的影响多于女性,肛门癌和生殖器疣是很重要的疾病,但往往没有得到充分讨论。它强调了 疫苗 在预防这些疾病中的作用,尽管它承认并非所有 HPV 毒株都会导致癌症,并且并非所有病例都与 HPV 相关。
应对、状态与不确定性
本文提供了有关 HPV 疫苗 及其益处的一般信息,但没有具体提及正在进行的调查、测试或公共卫生应对措施。它被视为公共卫生解释,而不是 暴发 或事件的报告。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Beyond cervical cancer: 5 diseases the HPV vaccine can actually prevent - theweek.in

News Brief
The article published on August 09, 2026, by Dr. Aruna Kalra highlights that the HPV vaccine offers protection against diseases beyond cervical cancer, including oropharyngeal cancers, anal cancer, vulvar and vaginal cancers, and genital warts. It emphasizes the importance of vaccinating adolescents before exposure to the virus, as there is no routine screening for throat cancer, making prevention through vaccination more critical. The piece also addresses the misconception that HPV vaccination is solely for women, noting that it is equally relevant for men. While the vaccine targets specific high-risk strains, it is effective in preventing a substantial proportion of these diseases. The article underscores the need for more accurate and inclusive public awareness about the vaccine's broader health implications.
Original source text (English)
SECTIONS journalism with a human touch Login My Profile Signed In as Profile Change Password Sign out Home Magazine Quick Read India World Sports Business Entertainment Reviews Columns Leisure More Hamburger Web Stories Videos Sci/Tech Health Defence Middle East Maritime Tourism Education Jobs & Career Search Subscribe Specials Middle East Defence Health Maritime × Search results for journalism with a human touch × News India World Sports Business Tourism Sci/Tech Entertainment Jobs & Career Magazine The Week Health Opinion Specials Health Defence Middle East Maritime Reviews Movies Books Gadgets Games Multimedia Videos Web Stories Quick Read Leisure Society Lifestyle Services About Us Privacy Policy Terms of Use Contact Us SIGN IN SUBSCRIBE Follow Us News Health Beyond cervical cancer: 5 diseases the HPV vaccine can actually prevent HPV vaccine benefits extend well beyond cervical cancer, offering crucial protection against a range of other cancers and conditions in all genders Dr Aruna Kalra Published: August 09, 2026 11:27 AM IST 3 minute Read Share × Email Facebook Twitter WhatsApp LinkedIn Telegram Reddit 0 Comments Link Copied Highlights Vaccination is most effective when administered to adolescents before any exposure to the virus Unlike cervical cancer, there is no routine screening for throat cancer, making vaccination the most effective form of prevention for boys and men HPV is a leading cause of throat cancers, which now affect more men than women have cervical cancer in some countries Ask most people what the HPV vaccine is for, and the answer will almost always be the same: cervical cancer. It is not a wrong answer — but it is an incomplete one, and doctors say this narrow understanding is quietly costing lives. Human papillomavirus is not a women's health issue confined to one organ. It is a common virus that moves silently between people, and its consequences reach far wider than most patients, and even some clinicians in general conversation, tend to acknowledge. The vaccine was introduced under the banner of cervical cancer prevention, and understandably so — it remains the most visible and most studied benefit, a practising physician explains. But that framing has, in some ways, boxed the vaccine into a single narrative. Parents of sons often assume it isn't relevant to them. That assumption is simply incorrect. Here is what the conversation usually leaves out. 1. Oropharyngeal (throat) cancers HPV is now recognised as a leading cause of cancers at the back of the throat, tonsils and base of the tongue — and in several countries, these cases in men have overtaken cervical cancer cases in women. Unlike cervical cancer, there is no routine screening test for throat cancer, which makes prevention through vaccination considerably more valuable, not less. 2. Anal cancer Anal cancer linked to HPV affects both men and women, though certain groups carry a disproportionately higher risk. It is rarely discussed openly, largely due to stigma rather than rarity, and doctors argue that this silence is precisely why awareness campaigns tend to skip over it. 3. Penile cancer A less common but genuinely preventable disease, penile cancer is strongly associated with HPV infection. Its omission from most public vaccine messaging reinforces the myth that HPV vaccination is a "female" intervention, when the virus itself makes no such distinction. 4. Vulvar and vaginal cancers These cancers receive comparatively little attention, overshadowed entirely by their more widely publicised cervical counterpart. Yet a substantial proportion of cases are directly attributable to HPV, and are equally preventable through timely vaccination. 5. Genital warts Not life-threatening, but far from trivial. Genital warts caused by HPV can be persistent, distressing and costly to treat, and they affect a meaningful share of sexually active adults. Doctors note that this is often the first, and sometimes only, tangible benefit patients notice after vaccination programmes take hold in a population. The consultant is careful to add a note of nuance here: not every strain of HPV causes cancer, and not every case of cancer at these sites is HPV-related. "The vaccine isn't a blanket shield against all disease at these locations — it targets specific high-risk strains. But those strains do the overwhelming share of the damage, which is exactly why prevention matters so much." What ties all five conditions together, doctors stress, is timing. The vaccine works best when given before exposure to the virus — typically recommended for adolescents of any gender, well before the onset of sexual activity — though catch-up vaccination remains worthwhile for many young adults. Perhaps the most persistent myth of all is that HPV vaccination is a women's health decision. It isn't. It is a shared, preventable-disease conversation that concerns sons as much as daughters, and one that deserves to be had earlier, more openly, and with considerably more accuracy than it currently is. The author is the Director - Obstetrics & Gynaecology, Director Academics - Robotic Gynaecology, CK Birla Hospital, Gurgaon. Time
The article was published on August 09, 2026, and discusses the HPV vaccine's benefits in preventing various diseases, including cancers and genital warts, with implications for vaccination timing and public awareness.
Location and population
The article is general in nature and does not specify a particular location or population. It discusses the HPV vaccine's role in preventing diseases across all genders and populations.
Event
The article explains that the HPV vaccine prevents diseases beyond cervical cancer, such as oropharyngeal cancers, anal cancer, vulvar and vaginal cancers, and genital warts. It highlights the importance of vaccination before exposure to the virus and addresses misconceptions about its relevance to males.
Scale, impact and risk
The article notes that HPV-associated oropharyngeal cancers now affect more men than women in some countries, and that anal cancer and genital warts are significant but often under-discussed conditions. It emphasizes the vaccine's role in preventing these diseases, though it acknowledges that not all HPV strains cause cancer and not all cases are HPV-related.
Response, status and uncertainty
The article provides general information about the HPV vaccine and its benefits, with no specific mention of ongoing investigations, testing, or public health responses. It is framed as a public health explanation rather than a report of an outbreak or event.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

万泰生物(603392):万泰生物关于公司九价HPV疫苗获得泰国上市许可 - 中财网

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报2026年8月11日,北京万泰生物制药企业宣布,其厦门子公司生产的九价HPV 疫苗已获得泰国监管机构的上市许可。基于大肠杆菌的 疫苗 针对 9 种致癌和低风险 HPV 类型,适用于 9-45 岁的女性。这标志着该产品首次获得海外批准,并支持该公司进军东南亚市场。万泰表示,此次批准加强了其全球 疫苗 网络和品牌影响力,同时指出,由于政策、市场和竞争因素,未来的销售和其他国家注册仍存在不确定性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表于 2026-08-11 16:56:34;所提供的证据中未明确报告事件日期。
地点与对象
泰国;公司总部位于中国北京;位于中国厦门的子公司。
事件
北京万泰生物制药企业股份有限公司宣布,其全资子公司厦门万泰沧海生物科技有限公司研发的九价HPV 疫苗(大肠杆菌型)获得泰国食品药品监督管理局上市许可。
规模、影响与风险
不适用于监管批准公告;没有病例、死亡或暴露的报告。疫苗 针对 HPV 6、11、16、18、31、33、45、52 和 58 型,用于预防 9-45 岁女性的相关疾病。
应对、状态与不确定性
获得监管部门批准;公司计划继续提交海外注册申请;未来的销售和批准仍然受到政策、市场和竞争不确定性的影响。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
万泰生物(603392):万泰生物关于公司九价HPV疫苗获得泰国上市许可 - 中财网
News Brief
On 11 August 2026, Beijing Wantai Biological Pharmacy Enterprise announced that its nine-valent HPV vaccine, produced by its Xiamen subsidiary, has received marketing authorization from Thailand’s regulator. The Escherichia coli–based vaccine targets nine oncogenic and low-risk HPV types and is indicated for females aged 9–45. This marks the first overseas approval for the product and supports the company’s push to expand into Southeast Asia. Wantai stated the approval strengthens its global vaccine network and brand presence, while noting that future sales and additional country registrations remain uncertain due to policy, market, and competitive factors.
Original source text (Chinese)

近日,北京万泰生物药业股份有限公司(以下简称“公司”)全资子公司厦门万泰沧海生物技术有限公司(以下简称“万泰沧海”)自主研发的九价人乳头瘤病毒疫苗(大肠埃希菌)(以下简称“九价HPV疫苗”)获得泰国食品药品监督管理局批准。现将相关信息公告如下: 一、药品基本信息 药品名称:九价人乳头瘤病毒疫苗(大肠埃希菌)(英文名称:RecombinantHuman Papillomavirus 9-Valent(Types 6,11,16,18,31,33,45,52,58)Vaccine(Escherichiacoli) 剂型:注射剂 规格:0.5mL/剂 二、药品其他信息 九价HPV疫苗为万泰沧海自主研发的预防用生物制品,已于2025年获得国家药品监督管理局颁发的药品注册证书,为我国首款、全球第二款九价HPV疫苗,主要适用于9-45岁女性,覆盖HPV16/18/31/33/45/52/58七种高危型和HPV6/11两种低危型,可预防上述型别人乳头瘤病毒感染引起的相关疾病。 三、对公司的影响及风险提示 本次九价HPV疫苗在泰国获批,是该产品取得的首个境外上市许可,标志着该产品已具备出口至泰国市场的资质。本次获批不仅为公司在泰国市场的业务拓展奠定了坚实基础,更将对公司持续拓展东南亚市场产生积极而深远的影响。 结合公司二价HPV疫苗已取得的国际化成果,公司正构建从二价到九价、从公共卫生采购到商业化销售的全球化疫苗网络,进一步提升公司在国际市场的品牌影响力和竞争力。 然而,九价HPV疫苗未来在境外的销售情况可能受到政策环境变化、市场推广、客户需求、市场竞争等多重因素影响,存在不确定性。 公司将持续推进九价HPV疫苗在海外国家的注册申报,相关申报能否获得批准及获得批准的时间尚存在不确定性。公司未来将按照相关规定在定期报告中披露在相关市场获得上市许可的情况。 敬请广大投资者谨慎决策,注意防范投资风险。 特此公告。 北京万泰生物药业股份有限公司董事会 Published 2026-08-11 16:56:34; event date not explicitly reported in the supplied evidence.

Thailand; company headquarters in Beijing, China; subsidiary in Xiamen, China.
Event
The Beijing Wantai Biological Pharmacy Enterprise Co., Ltd. announced that its nine-valent HPV vaccine (Escherichia coli–based) developed by its wholly owned subsidiary, Xiamen Wantai Canghai Biotechnology Co., Ltd., received marketing authorization from Thailand’s Food and Drug Administration.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

科学家研制出口香糖,可以保护高危人群免受口腔癌和咽喉癌的侵害 - 纽约邮报

Scientists create chewing gum that could protect high-risk people from mouth and throat cancer - New York Post
新闻简报宾夕法尼亚大学牙科医学院的科学家们开发出了一种由拉布拉豆制成的生物工程口香糖,可以帮助保护高危人群免受口腔癌和咽喉癌的侵害。这种口香糖显着降低了 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 和两种细菌牙龈卟啉单胞菌 (Pg) 和具核梭杆菌 (Fn) 的水平,这两种细菌与鳞状细胞癌有关。在实验室测试中,口香糖将唾液样本中的 HPV 降低了 93%,将口腔冲洗液样本中的 HPV 降低了 80%。添加抗菌肽 protegrin 后,可将 Pg 和 Fn 水平降低至接近零,而不影响有益的口腔细菌。这项发表在《科学报告》上的研究表明,这种口香糖可以与目前的 治疗 一起用作辅助治疗或预防措施。研究人员呼吁进行临床试验以进一步探索其潜力。此前的研究强调了口腔卫生在降低癌症风险方面的重要性,而且美国癌症协会指出,生活方式的改变可以预防 30 岁以上成年人 40% 的癌症病例。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于 2026 年发表,新闻报道于 2026 年 8 月 10 日发表。
地点与对象
该研究是在宾夕法尼亚大学牙科医学院进行的,针对口腔癌和咽喉癌的高危人群,特别是那些感染 高危型HPV 型(例如 HPV 16、HPV 18 和 HPV 59)的人群。
事件
科学家开发了一种由扁豆制成的生物工程口香糖,可显着降低 HPV 和两种与鳞状细胞癌(头颈癌的常见形式)相关的细菌(牙龈卟啉单胞菌和具核梭杆菌)的水平。
规模、影响与风险
该口香糖可将唾液样本中的 HPV 降低 93%,将口腔冲洗液样本中的 HPV 降低 80%。添加 protegrin 后,Pg 和 Fn 水平几乎降至零。这些发现表明有可能作为 HPV 相关癌症的辅助治疗或预防措施。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究发表在《科学报告》上,研究人员正在倡导进行临床试验来评估其作为辅助或预防性治疗的用途。该证据基于实验室测试,不包括临床试验结果或实际应用数据。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Scientists create chewing gum that could protect high-risk people from mouth and throat cancer - New York Post

News Brief
Scientists at the University of Pennsylvania School of Dental Medicine have developed a bioengineered chewing gum made from lablab beans that could help protect high-risk individuals from mouth and throat cancer. The gum significantly reduced levels of human papillomavirus (HPV) and two bacteria, Porphyromonas gingivalis (Pg) and Fusobacterium nucleatum (Fn), which are linked to squamous cell carcinoma. In laboratory tests, the gum reduced HPV by 93% in saliva samples and 80% in oral rinse samples. When protegrin, an antimicrobial peptide, was added, it reduced Pg and Fn levels to nearly zero without affecting beneficial oral bacteria. The study, published in Scientific Reports, suggests the gum could be used as an adjuvant therapy or preventive measure alongside current treatments. Researchers are calling for clinical trials to further explore its potential. This development comes as previous studies highlight the importance of oral hygiene in reducing cancer risk, and the American Cancer Society notes that lifestyle changes could prevent 40% of cancer cases in adults over 30.
Original source text (English)

Health Scientists create chewing gum that could protect high-risk people from mouth and throat cancer By Rachel Sacks Published Aug. 10, 2026, 1:43 p.m. ET Comments 1/1 Skip Ad Continue watchingafter the adVisit Advertiser websiteGO TO PAGE See more of our coverage in your search results. Add The New York Post on Google Chew on this news. Scientists may have developed an easy way to stop one of the most aggressive and common forms of head and neck cancer that often starts in the mouth and throat. And all it would take is popping a piece of bioengineered bean gum. Reducing one of the most aggressive and common head and neck cancers may be as easy as popping a piece of gum. Africa Studio – stock.adobe.com Accounting for 90% of all head and neck cancers, squamous cell carcinoma is the second most common type of skin cancer in the US. However, many current treatments have shown limited effectiveness, prompting researchers at the School of Dental Medicine at the University of Pennsylvania to create a better therapy. The result, published in Scientific Reports, was a bioengineered gum made from lablab beans — a species of bean also known as hyacinth beans — that sharply reduced one virus and two types of bacteria linked to the disease. Building on previous work creating a chewing gum made from bean gum and containing a naturally antiviral protein FRIL, researchers tested oral samples of patients for three microbes associated with cancer. These included human papillomavirus (HPV) and two bacterial species, Porphyromonas gingivalis (Pg) and Fusobacterium nucleatum (Fn). The increase in mouth and throat cancer has been strongly linked to HPV infection, accounting for 60% to 75% of cases. High-risk HPV types like HPV 16, HPV 18 and HPV 59 are the most likely to result in squamous carcinoma. A bioengineered bean gum greatly reduced levels of one virus and two types of bacteria linked to squamous cell cancer. Kevin Monko/Penn Dental Medicine “And Pg and Fn infections worsen survival rates of untreated recurrent or metastatic oral cancer, even after surgery and risk-adjusted adjuvant, or supplemental, therapies,” lead researcher Henry Daniell said in a press release. The bean gum extracts reduced HPV by 93% in saliva samples and 80% in oral rinse samples. When protegrin, an antimicrobial peptide that kills harmful bacteria, was added to the gum, a single dose reduced Pg and Fn levels to nearly zero. Notably, the addition of protegrin also didn’t affect beneficial bacteria found in the mouth, contrasting with radiation therapy, which reduces good bacteria and increases disease-causing yeast. Researchers believe the gum’s ability to target dangerous microbes while preserving healthy oral microbiome could be useful as an additional therapy along with current treatments or as a preventive measure. “Our findings support the value of advancing these therapies to clinical trials as adjuvants with current treatments or as prophylaxis to prevent infection and transmission,” Daniell said. Previous studies have found that daily oral maintenance — regularly brushing and flossing — could drastically reduce the chances of developing head and neck cancers. What do you think? Post a comment. These healthy habits could potentially reduce more than a dozen species of bacteria that increase the risk of squamous cell carcinoma. This came on the heels of the American Cancer Society declaring that 40% of cancer cases and nearly half of cancer deaths in American adults 30 and older could be prevented with a lifestyle change. Scientists developed a bioengineered chewing gum made from lablab beans that significantly reduced levels of HPV and two bacteria (Porphyromonas gingivalis and Fusobacterium nucleatum) linked to squamous cell carcinoma, a common form of head and neck cancer.
Scale, impact and risk
The gum reduced HPV by 93% in saliva samples and 80% in oral rinse samples. When protegrin was added, it reduced Pg and Fn levels to nearly zero. These findings suggest potential as an adjuvant therapy or preventive measure for HPV-associated cancers.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study was published in Scientific Reports, and the researchers are advocating for clinical trials to evaluate its use as an adjuvant or prophylactic therapy. The evidence is based on laboratory testing and does not include clinical trial results or real-world application data.
